I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sleep is… I’ve never been one of those people who just puts their head down and disappears for eight hours. My sister can do that, which always amazed me—she could nap through the vacuum cleaner.

Some nights I lie there thinking about little things, but then I’ll also get distracted by whether I remembered to put the laundry in the dryer, or what time the store delivery comes in, and it’s hard to say if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just me being me.

So… I guess I haven’t really tracked it. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.

was convinced I could fix the whole store’s image myself and that the district manager just couldn’t see how brilliant my ideas were.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… more than two weeks in a row is such a specific cutoff, isn’t it? district manager questioned my layouts.\n- key: S034\n  name: Increased_Activity\n  description: Abnormally and persistently increased goal-directed activity or energy.\n  manifestation: For a while I had this burst of energy where I couldn’t sit still after work and kept sketching store ideas, reorganizing shelves at home, and signing up for extra training I didn’t really need.\n- key: S035\n  name: Inflated_Self_Esteem\n  description: Inflated self-esteem or grandiosity (may range from uncritical self-confidence to delusional proportions).\n  manifestation: During that energized spell, I was convinced I could fix the whole store’s image myself and that the district manager just couldn’t see how brilliant my ideas were.\n- key: S039\n  name: Manic_Distractibility\n  description: Attention is too easily drawn to unimportant or irrelevant external stimuli.\n  manifestation: When I was in that keyed-up mode, I’d start one planogram and then get pulled away by a customer’s scarf color, a song on the speaker, or a new idea popping into my head.\n- key: S040\n  name: Increased_Goal_Activity\n  description: Increase in goal-directed activity (at work, school, socially, or sexually) or purposeless psychomotor agitation.\n  manifestation: I was emailing coworkers after hours, proposing new cultural promotion displays, offering to mentor extra staff, and filling notebook manifestation: This whole up-and-down stretch with my mood and energy has been going on for about two months, since the trouble with the new district manager really started.\n- key: depressive_episode_duration\n  name: Depressive Episode Duration\n  description: 6 months\n  manifestation: The low, flat part has been present for about six months now, long enough that my usual I mean, I know I’ve had times where I felt off or sort of uninterested, but then I’ll remember some birthday dinner or some thing at work that seemed okay, and then I start doubting my own memory.\n\nThere were stretches, maybe, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was more than two weeks,” not with any confidence. I don’t keep track of moods the way I keep track of sales reports or appointments. So… I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear one I can point to before this.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I really can’t pin one down. Nothing before this stands out clearly enough for me to say yes, that was definitely the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a couple times, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. It’s silly, probably, but I’ll get stuck replaying these tiny things from work. I mean, retail is always messy—people move things, customers ask the same question three different ways, and the seasonal aisle never stays neat, not even for an hour—but somehow I make it about me. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n12\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.1, F32.2, F33.1, F33.2\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. Can you tell me what brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well… I guess what finally scared me into actually making the appointment is, I’ve been having these thoughts like, if I didn’t wake up tomorrow, maybe that would be easier. I don’t mean I want to hurt myself—I don’t, really—but the thought keeps showing up, and it made me think maybe I shouldn’t just keep brushing it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I still try. I’ll put my paints out on the table, or I’ll tell myself I’m going to go to pottery class, because that used to be my little reset, you know? I even bought this really nice blue glaze once—well, I guess that’s not really the point.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, I just kind of sit and look at the clay or the brushes, and there’s just… nothing. Like the spark I used to get from it isn’t there. It feels strange, because I know I used to love it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and do you notice that your mood has been consistently low or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Most mornings lately, yeah… I wake up and it’s like this heavy, gray feeling is already there, before I even get dressed for the store. It’s funny, I used to be so particular about what blouse I wore to work—customers notice those things, you know—but lately it’s like the color has drained out of everything before the day even starts. So… yes, I’d say my mood has been pretty low most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not good enough, even when there’s no real reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s silly, probably, but I’ll get stuck replaying these tiny things from work. Like if a display sign was a little crooked, or one of the new trainees looked confused after I explained something, I’ll go home and think, “Well, that’s because I’m useless as a supervisor.”\n\nAnd then I’ll start remembering every other time I didn’t handle something perfectly. I mean, retail is always messy—people move things, customers ask the same question three different ways, and the seasonal aisle never stays neat, not even for an hour—but somehow I make it about me. Like I should’ve done better, even when it’s not really a big deal.\n\nDoctor: How long have these feelings of low mood, loss of interest, and guilt been going on?\n\nPatient: I think… about six months, give or take. It’s hard to pin down exactly because it didn’t arrive all at once, you know? It was more like my morning walks stopped helping, and then pottery class felt like another thing on the calendar instead of something I looked forward to.\n\nI remember thinking maybe it was just a bad week at work, or the weather, or that I needed to reorganize the kitchen cabinets—because I do that when I’m trying to feel in control—but no, the low, flat feeling has been there for months now. Around six months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like waking up much earlier than you used to or having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sleep is… I’ve never been one of those people who just puts their head down and disappears for eight hours. My sister can do that, which always amazed me—she could nap through the vacuum cleaner.\n\nLately, I don’t know that I’ve noticed a clear pattern like waking up at some exact awful hour or anything. Some nights I lie there thinking about little things, but then I’ll also get distracted by whether I remembered to put the laundry in the dryer, or what time the store delivery comes in, and it’s hard to say if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just me being me.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really tracked it. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt this way before, or is this the first time you’ve experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I can say cleanly. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone has, and sometimes I’ll think, “Was that the same thing?” But then I start comparing it to, you know, when my kids were younger or when work was especially hectic, and it gets all tangled up.\n\nThere were times I felt worn down, sure, but whether it was this same kind of low, flat feeling… I’m not really sure. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… more than two weeks in a row is such a specific cutoff, isn’t it? I mean, I know I’ve had times where I felt off or sort of uninterested, but then I’ll remember some birthday dinner or some thing at work that seemed okay, and then I start doubting my own memory.\n\nThere were stretches, maybe, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was more than two weeks,” not with any confidence. I don’t keep track of moods the way I keep track of sales reports or appointments. So… I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear one I can point to before this.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I really can’t pin one down. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt badly about myself or wasn’t enjoying much, but then I think about all the normal ups and downs—work stress, family stuff, getting older, even just bad weather for a week—and it blurs together.\n\nTwo weeks or more… I honestly don’t know. Nothing before this stands out clearly enough for me to say yes, that was definitely the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a couple times, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I just… I can’t think of a clean example from before this.\n\nThere were periods where I was tired or kind of discouraged, but life was busy, you know? In retail there’s always some season that wipes you out—holidays, inventory, someone quitting right before a big weekend—and then at home there’s always something too. So I have trouble separating “I was stressed and worn out” from “I was really down for two weeks.”\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a clear time before this six months that I can point to and say that was definitely it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, can you think of any time before this current six-month period when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and maybe felt guilty or not good enough?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably circling the drain on this, but I still don’t have a clear example. It’s funny, I used to be so particular about what blouse I wore to work—customers notice those things, you know—but lately it’s like the color has drained out of everything before the day even starts. Like if a display sign was a little crooked, or one of the new trainees looked confused after I explained something, I’ll go home and think, “Well, that’s because I’m useless as a supervisor.”\n\nAnd then I’ll start remembering every other time I didn’t handle something perfectly. Some nights I lie there thinking about little things, but then I’ll also get distracted by whether I remembered to put the laundry in the dryer, or what time the store delivery comes in, and it’s hard to say if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just me being me.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really tracked it. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt this way before, or is this the first time you’ve experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I can say cleanly. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone has, and sometimes I’ll think, “Was that the same thing?” But then I start comparing it to, you know, when my kids were younger or when work was especially hectic, and it gets all tangled up.\n\nThere were times I felt worn down, sure, but whether it was this same kind of low, flat feeling… I’m not really sure. Nothing before this stands out clearly enough for me to say yes, that was definitely the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a couple times, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I mean, I know I’ve had times where I felt off or sort of uninterested, but then I’ll remember some birthday dinner or some thing at work that seemed okay, and then I start doubting my own memory.\n\nThere were stretches, maybe, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was more than two weeks,” not with any confidence. I don’t keep track of moods the way I keep track of sales reports or appointments. So… I’m not sure. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I’m sorry, I know you’re asking it very clearly, and I keep circling around it. Before these last six months… I just don’t have a clean memory of a two-week stretch like that.

There were times I felt lousy or like I wasn’t doing enough, but then I start attaching it to work—holiday rush, staffing problems, all the usual store chaos—and I can’t honestly say, yes, that was most days for two weeks and I lost interest in things. Like the spark I used to get from it isn’t there. It’s funny, I used to be so particular about what blouse I wore to work—customers notice those things, you know—but lately it’s like the color has drained out of everything before the day even starts. It’s silly, probably, but I’ll get stuck replaying these tiny things from work. I mean, retail is always messy—people move things, customers ask the same question three different ways, and the seasonal aisle never stays neat, not even for an hour—but somehow I make it about me. Like I should’ve done better, even when it’s not really a big deal.\n\nDoctor: How long have these feelings of low mood, loss of interest, and guilt been going on?\n\nPatient: I think… about six months, give or take. It’s hard to pin down exactly because it didn’t arrive all at once, you know? It was more like my morning walks stopped helping, and then pottery class felt like another thing on the calendar instead of something I looked forward to.\n\nI remember thinking maybe it was just a bad week at work, or the weather, or that I needed to reorganize the kitchen cabinets—because I do that when I’m trying to feel in control—but no, the low, flat feeling has been there for months now. Around six months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like waking up much earlier than you used to or having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sleep is… I’ve never been one of those people who just puts their head down and disappears for eight hours. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt this way before, or is this the first time you’ve experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I can say cleanly. It’s funny, I used to be so particular about what blouse I wore to work—customers notice those things, you know—but lately it’s like the color has drained out of everything before the day even starts. So… yes, I’d say my mood has been pretty low most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not good enough, even when there’s no real reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s silly, probably, but I’ll get stuck replaying these tiny things from work. Like if a display sign was a little crooked, or one of the new trainees looked confused after I explained something, I’ll go home and think, “Well, that’s because I’m useless as a supervisor.”\n\nAnd then I’ll start remembering every other time I didn’t handle something perfectly. I mean, retail is always messy—people move things, customers ask the same question three different ways, and the seasonal aisle never stays neat, not even for an hour—but somehow I make it about me. It’s hard to pin down exactly because it didn’t arrive all at once, you know? It was more like my morning walks stopped helping, and then pottery class felt like another thing on the calendar instead of something I looked forward to.\n\nI remember thinking maybe it was just a bad week at work, or the weather, or that I needed to reorganize the kitchen cabinets—because I do that when I’m trying to feel in control—but no, the low, flat feeling has been there for months now. Around six months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like waking up much earlier than you used to or having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sleep is… I’ve never been one of those people who just puts their head down and disappears for eight hours. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt this way before, or is this the first time you’ve experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I can say cleanly. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone has, and sometimes I’ll think, “Was that the same thing?” But then I start comparing it to, you know, when my kids were younger or when work was especially hectic, and it gets all tangled up.\n\nThere were times I felt worn down, sure, but whether it was this same kind of low, flat feeling… I’m not really sure. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… more than two weeks in a row is such a specific cutoff, isn’t it? I mean, I know I’ve had times where I felt off or sort of uninterested, but then I’ll remember some birthday dinner or some thing at work that seemed okay, and then I start doubting my own memory.\n\nThere were stretches, maybe, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was more than two weeks,” not with any confidence. I don’t keep track of moods the way I keep track of sales reports or appointments. So… I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear one I can point to before this.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I really can’t pin one down. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt badly about myself or wasn’t enjoying much, but then I think about all the normal ups and downs—work stress, family stuff, getting older, even just bad weather for a week—and it blurs together.\n\nTwo weeks or more… I honestly don’t know. Nothing before this stands out clearly enough for me to say yes, that was definitely the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a couple times, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I just… I can’t think of a clean example from before this.\n\nThere were periods where I was tired or kind of discouraged, but life was busy, you know? In retail there’s always some season that wipes you out—holidays, inventory, someone quitting right before a big weekend—and then at home there’s always something too. So I have trouble separating “I was stressed and worn out” from “I was really down for two weeks.”\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a clear time before this six months that I can point to and say that was definitely it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, can you think of any time before this current six-month period when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and maybe felt guilty or not good enough?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably circling the drain on this, but I still don’t have a clear example. I remember being tired and frustrated, but I can’t really separate out whether I was down most days and not enjoying things for two full weeks. So… before these last six months, I don’t remember a clear stretch like that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, even if it wasn’t recent, can you think of any time when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and felt guilty or not good enough?\n\nPatient: Lately, yes… I mean, not really looking back years ago, but lately most mornings I wake up and it’s like this heavy gray feeling is already there. Like the color’s drained out of everything before I even get dressed to go to the store.\n\nAnd then I start thinking about opening routines and whether someone called out, and I know that’s not exactly what you asked, but it’s all mixed together in my head. The clearest thing I can say is that lately, the mornings have felt really down like that.\n\n[Last Question]\nLooking back over your life, even if it wasn’t recent, can you think of any time when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and felt guilty or not good enough?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 62 / first_line_supervisor_of_retail_sales_worker / bachelors\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Depressed_Mood\nmanifestation: \"Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like the color has drained out before I even get dressed for the store.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says.   },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I know you’re asking if it happened sometime way back, but the clearest thing I can point to is more recent. Most mornings lately I wake up with this heavy, gray feeling, like everything’s already sort of drained of color before I even get dressed for the store.

And then my mind goes right to the store schedule and who’s going to be late, and I get off track thinking about that, but… the mood part, the down feeling, that’s been there in the mornings.

I don’t mean I want to hurt myself—I don’t, really—but the thought keeps showing up, and it made me think maybe I shouldn’t just keep brushing it off.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you used to enjoy, like hobbies or activities?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I mean, I still try. I’ll put my paints out on the table, or I’ll tell myself I’m going to go to pottery class, because that used to be my little reset, you know? I even bought this really nice blue glaze once—well, I guess that’s not really the point.\n\nBut when I’m actually there, I just kind of sit and look at the clay or the brushes, and there’s just… nothing. Like the spark I used to get from it isn’t there. It feels strange, because I know I used to love it.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way, and do you notice that your mood has been consistently low or depressed most days?\n\nPatient: Most mornings lately, yeah… I wake up and it’s like this heavy, gray feeling is already there, before I even get dressed for the store. It’s funny, I used to be so particular about what blouse I wore to work—customers notice those things, you know—but lately it’s like the color has drained out of everything before the day even starts. So… yes, I’d say my mood has been pretty low most days.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself feeling guilty or like you’re not good enough, even when there’s no real reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: Yes… I do. It’s silly, probably, but I’ll get stuck replaying these tiny things from work. Like if a display sign was a little crooked, or one of the new trainees looked confused after I explained something, I’ll go home and think, “Well, that’s because I’m useless as a supervisor.”\n\nAnd then I’ll start remembering every other time I didn’t handle something perfectly. I mean, retail is always messy—people move things, customers ask the same question three different ways, and the seasonal aisle never stays neat, not even for an hour—but somehow I make it about me. Like I should’ve done better, even when it’s not really a big deal.\n\nDoctor: How long have these feelings of low mood, loss of interest, and guilt been going on?\n\nPatient: I think… about six months, give or take. It’s hard to pin down exactly because it didn’t arrive all at once, you know? It was more like my morning walks stopped helping, and then pottery class felt like another thing on the calendar instead of something I looked forward to.\n\nI remember thinking maybe it was just a bad week at work, or the weather, or that I needed to reorganize the kitchen cabinets—because I do that when I’m trying to feel in control—but no, the low, flat feeling has been there for months now. Around six months, I’d say.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your sleep lately, like waking up much earlier than you used to or having trouble falling asleep?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure, honestly. I mean, sleep is… I’ve never been one of those people who just puts their head down and disappears for eight hours. My sister can do that, which always amazed me—she could nap through the vacuum cleaner.\n\nLately, I don’t know that I’ve noticed a clear pattern like waking up at some exact awful hour or anything. Some nights I lie there thinking about little things, but then I’ll also get distracted by whether I remembered to put the laundry in the dryer, or what time the store delivery comes in, and it’s hard to say if that’s “trouble sleeping” or just me being me.\n\nSo… I guess I haven’t really tracked it. Nothing jumps out as a big change, I don’t think.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any other periods in your life where you felt this way before, or is this the first time you’ve experienced these feelings?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… I’m not sure I can say cleanly. I mean, I’ve had rough patches before, like anyone has, and sometimes I’ll think, “Was that the same thing?” But then I start comparing it to, you know, when my kids were younger or when work was especially hectic, and it gets all tangled up.\n\nThere were times I felt worn down, sure, but whether it was this same kind of low, flat feeling… I’m not really sure. I’d have to think about it more.\n\nDoctor: Can you recall if there was a time before this current period when you felt down or lost interest in things for more than two weeks in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m trying to think… more than two weeks in a row is such a specific cutoff, isn’t it? I mean, I know I’ve had times where I felt off or sort of uninterested, but then I’ll remember some birthday dinner or some thing at work that seemed okay, and then I start doubting my own memory.\n\nThere were stretches, maybe, but I can’t honestly say, “yes, that was more than two weeks,” not with any confidence. I don’t keep track of moods the way I keep track of sales reports or appointments. So… I’m not sure. I don’t really have a clear one I can point to before this.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I really can’t pin one down. I mean, I’ve had times where I felt badly about myself or wasn’t enjoying much, but then I think about all the normal ups and downs—work stress, family stuff, getting older, even just bad weather for a week—and it blurs together.\n\nTwo weeks or more… I honestly don’t know. Nothing before this stands out clearly enough for me to say yes, that was definitely the same kind of thing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any time in your life before this current six-month period when you felt down, lost interest in things, or had thoughts like you’re not good enough for a period of two weeks or more?\n\nPatient: I know you’ve asked it a couple times, and I’m sorry, I’m not trying to dodge it. I just… I can’t think of a clean example from before this.\n\nThere were periods where I was tired or kind of discouraged, but life was busy, you know? In retail there’s always some season that wipes you out—holidays, inventory, someone quitting right before a big weekend—and then at home there’s always something too. So I have trouble separating “I was stressed and worn out” from “I was really down for two weeks.”\n\nSo… no, I don’t have a clear time before this six months that I can point to and say that was definitely it.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, can you think of any time before this current six-month period when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and maybe felt guilty or not good enough?\n\nPatient: I’m sorry, I know I’m probably circling the drain on this, but I still don’t have a clear example. When I look back, I can remember being unhappy here and there, or just kind of worn thin, but then my mind goes off to what was happening at the time—like whether the store was short-staffed, or if I was just tired from all the running around.\n\nSo as far as a definite two-week stretch where I felt down most days and wasn’t interested in things, before these last six months… I really can’t say yes. I don’t remember it clearly enough to give you that.\n\nDoctor: Looking back over your life, even if it wasn’t recent, can you think of any time when you had a clear stretch of at least two weeks where you felt down most days, lost interest in things you usually enjoy, and felt guilty or not good enough?\n\nPatient: I still can’t really pull up an old stretch clearly, not in the way you’re asking.
